I referred to incommunicado and would like to make it clear that incommunicado is regulated in our legal system in Articles 509 and 527. In 2015 there was a legislative reform to implement, determine or extend the requirements for the adoption of this measure and even limit the rights that could in turn, worth repeating, be limited by the judicial authority. For my professional work I will comment that the application of incommunicado detention is true that it is limited not only to terrorist crimes, but to crimes where there is a perfectly structured criminal organization; it does not have to be exclusively for terrorist crimes. It is true, as you have indicated, that it applied to terrorism. Two are the purposes of incommunicado detention, essentially: to avoid a serious risk to the lives of third persons, and that is why it can be adopted, or also a serious risk to the ongoing judicial proceedings. But it must be made clear that in such situations incommunicado should be agreed by the judicial authority. Since the beginning, since the years 1982, 1983 and 1984 of which you spoke, the intervention of the judicial authority has already been necessary not only to agree on it, but to control it. There were already judicial decisions - the Constitutional Court has been mentioned here before, which has been, as it were, for many years our Criminal Prosecution Act of 1982 - and the Constitutional Court has been telling the judges how we had to apply any article of the Criminal Prosecution Act in terms of its compatibility with the Constitution. Among these parameters of compatibility is article 15 of the Constitution, which enshrines or defines the physical and moral integrity of persons and the prohibition of any ill-treatment, the prohibition of torture. And how should one act incommunicado? I think you are aware that incommunicado detention at that time implied that the judicial authority controlled detention while they were incommunicado in forensic police headquarters attached to the Ministry of Justice, they were not attached to the Ministry of the Interior. On two occasions - it seems important to me - they moved to the police headquarters, where they were with the detainee and he should or could make reference to any circumstances or to how the detention had developed. Not only was that judicial control, but it was and is also the institution of habeas corpus. Any family member or person could bring habeas corpus proceedings by referring to the illegality or that any violation of fundamental rights, physical integrity and moral integrity in the course of such detention could have occurred in this case. From the outset, the Spanish democratic courts continued to investigate the facts when they were reported or when possible or hypothetical torture was alleged. The methodological procedures of the CIS remain as they have been so far, making five improvements that further align the activity with scientific rigour. First, the results of secret voting projection models are not published; second, the sample size is increased in five hundred interviews improving reliability and validity; third, more data collection points and greater representativeness-sample checks are introduced into the sample design; fourth, the deadlines for presenting and disseminating the results have been shortened; and fifth, transparency on the procedures performed is increased. The answer is that the CIS now more than ever respects precisely the principles of scientific activity. The CIS carries out its investigations according to the same methodologies that have been used for many years, with proper control and supervision of all field work, which continue to be carried out by the same technicians as you always praised, and which make up the CIS field team in which no changes have been made. The recording and tabulation of the data is done by the same company as usual, and CIS samples have been raised, as I said, from 2500 interviews to 3000 in order to increase their reliability and reduce the theoretical margins of error and thus be able to compensate for the effect of the increase in indefinition and non-responses. ParlaMint-ES_2018-12-12-CD181212.u308	Thank you very much. For the defence of the amendments put forward by your group, Mr Lamuà Estañol is speaking on behalf of the Socialist Group.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-12-12-CD181212.u309	Thank you, President. As the proposer has just mentioned, this motion has taken a few days of work to generate spaces of agreement and consensus, which is welcome. It's of great importance to us. I just want to remind you that whoever was our environmental spokesman until recently has four GNPs approved in directions that are now also reflected in this motion, which is to be thanked for our part. They do not escape that the hydroelectric infrastructures of our country that began to be built during the first quarter of the twentieth century, in the case of the valleys of the Pyrenees, for example, mainly to supply industries of Catalonia or the Basque Country, were made by concessionary companies that cost the construction and commissioning of the plants, so, in return, they would receive a concession of operation for a certain time. At the end of the concession period, these plants would become part of the State. Almost a century after that start, concession periods have begun to expire; these plants are in practice fully amortized, well-maintained and operational. In other words, they are infrastructures that must revert to the public domain and that will be a good source of wealth generation for Spain. The State must reverse these infrastructures, some of which, today, still give small benefits to its concessionaries, even though some of them have exceeded the original deadline for the termination of concessions. It is time, therefore, for those benefits to start to reverse clearly and diaphanously in the territories that support them and in their environment as well. In our view, recovering these concessions must mean several things. Firstly, since water is a public property, it must be managed from the perspective of the general interest on the basis of the principle of basin unity. Such management, within the framework of a just energy transition, should also mean creating new opportunities for those territories. Secondly, the quality of the water, the maintenance of ecological flows and that all this is a guarantee of the maintenance of the different ecosystems and their biodiversity must be a priority that derives directly from this motion. Finally, it must also have an impact on the territorial restitution of the territories affected by having borne these infrastructures during that time, because in many cases it may have limited development even in some of these rural locations, where they already have difficulties added by comparison with more urban enclaves. Some data show that while adjacent territories have had more favourable development rates, all infrastructure derived from these power stations have in some cases slowed the benefits to these municipalities, and this is something that needs to be taken into account. In short, once the power stations are reversed, the public interest must prevail over the particular interests of electricity. We must ensure that these reversals are translated into an example of transparency and good management and that they contrast with some opacities that have obviously been taking place. Furthermore, we must be aware of the local impact of such reversals. The loss of income resulting from the extinction of the IAE, for example, will affect the income of many municipalities in small municipalities and their budgets on an ordinary basis. We must therefore also take whatever measures are necessary and in whatever areas are necessary to ensure that the wealth generated by these power stations also reverts in a just and justified way to those areas of our geography, which would also be the demonstration that we manage for a common good. I'm done. The rapporteur was talking about sovereignty and consensus and I think it is a good day to mention the consensus after the debate we had this morning. History teaches us that it is very difficult to reach a consensus and that it is more difficult to maintain it, but it also teaches us that in politics consensus is or should be one of the most precious treasures for any politician who wants to be proud of it. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

A hydroelectric power plant requires precision in its machinery such as that of a clock. ParlaMint-ES_2018-12-12-CD181212.u316	Thank you very much. Mrs Sagastizabal Unzetabarrenetxea has the floor for the Basque Parliamentary Group, PNV.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-12-12-CD181212.u317	Thank you, President. Pick it up on. The exploitation of hydroelectric jumps, of course, has had and is of great importance in the electrical system. The main common objectives of the motion are that the benefits of the power stations become public ownership without delay, after the expiration of the concessions, and that the resources obtained transferred to local institutions or to the autonomous communities be reversed with criteria of economic and social restitution in the affected territories, favoring a greater vertebration of the affected areas and supporting a process of energy transition towards a more sustainable model. Among other things, it provides for the development of a stable legal framework for the reversal of hydroelectric leaps and proposes a procedure for the reversal prior to the end of the concession. In this regard, it proposes that the reverse cases be initiated a few years before the concessions in their respective territories expire and are resolved within one year. It should be said that the large part of concessions granted between the fifties and seventies and the model of exploitation of them is based on the fact that, in exchange for the particular investments necessary for the construction of the jumps, the Administration granted to the promoters a concession of exploitation of the waters with a duration of seventy-five years, subject to the condition that, at the end of the period, the facilities would revert to the State. The Ministry of the Environment in 2010 revised the fees for hydropower plants operating in the form of concession, as investments made were largely considered to be amortized; even formulas were considered to recover some concessions from hydropower plants before their expiry, something legally complicated and burdensome, especially for the compensation for loss of profits that should be paid to the concessionaires. As we have stated, in accordance with the applicable sectoral rules, the expiry of concessions for the eight hundred State hydroelectric power stations operated by private companies should, where appropriate, entail their reversal to the State. However, different governments at the national level throughout several legislatures as well as different public administrations have been pointed out for failing to process or over-extend the expiry files of some concessions upon expiry. The first expiry and subsequent public recovery of a hydroelectric jump in Spain did not occur until 2013, and came with much delay and delay, which caused the concessionaire to continue operating the power plant and obtaining revenues beyond the concession period. Therefore, the motion under analysis aims to avoid these undesirable administrative situations and, since from this year 2018 a succession of concessions will be expected to reach forty-seven in a decade, we understand that it is time to review the role of hydrological exploitation. Once the motion has been analyzed, we understand that a measure that could be applied, in line with what the ministry itself is working on, would be the possibility of recovering some concessions as they win and taking them out at public auction. It would even be reasonable to see what was proposed by the Sustainability Observatory, which recommends the recovery of all hydroelectric uses and rebidding them or not, but certainly under favourable conditions for the common interest. We understand that expired concessions should go to competition, a competition that should be settled before the expiry of the concession and in which even a period of learning should be allowed if there was a new successful tenderer. All this, to avoid above all the stoppage of installations at the end of the concessional period - which is what is currently happening - as this causes a very significant deterioration of the installations. However, we have to say that the experience we have had in the societies in which we have participated suggests that it is perhaps very difficult for the municipalities to manage these facilities. And with regard to the time limits for concessions, we understand that, despite the fact that the motion establishes a four-year period, it is a matter that should be discussed as long as it is a time-limit that is conditional on the realization of investments, although it must be said that the transactional period increases this time-limit by five years. Another issue that needs to be considered is that, following the reversal in the cases of concessions already declared obsolete, since the State is the holder of the holding is exempt from the payment of the tax on economic activities, which is currently paid by the hydroelectric companies, which may have a significant negative economic impact on municipal revenues. Perhaps it would be possible to think not only of the exemption from that tax, but of an economic compensation to the municipalities that stop receiving these revenues. Having said all this, we understand that this motion contains key points with which we agree, but we also understand that there are issues that need to be addressed in greater depth. We will wait to position ourselves until tomorrow, since there is an aspect in point 5 of the transactional that speaks of the obligation of the plants to follow the slogans of the system operator, that is, it orders, gives no option and therefore can be understood as a control. We also have doubts about Directive 2009/72, which provides that the same person or persons cannot exercise control over a generation or supply undertaking and at the same time exercise control or any right over a transmission system operator or a transmission system. Similarly, control over a transmission system or a transmission system operator should exclude the possibility of exercising control or any right over a generation or supply undertaking. Thank you so much.

ParlaMint-ES_2018-12-12-CD181212.u319	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I say to you that we are going to vote in favour of the motion tabled by the Confederal Group of the United People's Party on the review of hydraulic jumps, hydroelectric jumps which are currently managed by private companies, but also - let us remember - by entities of direct public management of a communal type or a public enterprise, although this is not really the problem. We should ask ourselves why the Government is currently being urged to regain direct management of a public domain property, water, whose ownership is ultimately public. The answer is that some are failing to comply with hydraulic legislation and concessional obligations, a regulation that precisely allows them to manage a public good of general interest, such as water. They believe in the full right of the use of jumps without taking into account the counter-performances corresponding to the management of a public good, the temporary nature of the concession and full transparency, two fundamental elements in order to be able to speak of a positive management. And they also confuse ownership and concessional law with interest in an attempt to patrimonialize and privatize water management. This is a perception of water management that ends up damaging the model we are talking about. But let's get to the bottom of it. Last Wednesday, September 26, the weekly El Temps published an investigation into the end of the concessions of hydroelectric power plants in the Catalan Pyrenees, owned by Endesa, and the continued exploitation of the company. The controversy arose as a result of a report by the Sustainability Observatory entitled Recovery of Hydroelectric Concessions. Two Italian companies, one with a majority public capital, Enel, and the other controlled by the Benetton family, Atlantis, decided to invest in Endesa and Abertis in 2009 knowing perfectly well that many of the concessions that protected their businesses were expired or about to expire. Even so, they did so with the aim of achieving an implicit or explicit extension of the time limits for the exploitation of concessions. This is what happens to the hydroelectric plants that ENEL has in the Pyrenees, inherited from the former companies Fecsa and Enher, which Endesa absorbed in 1999. Eight of the concessions that support its private operation - Cabdella, Seròs, Talarn, Camarasa, la Pobla, Sant Llorenç de Montgai, Gavet and Terradets, all in the regions of Lleida - are already expired, according to the maximum period of seventy-five years that marks the Water Act 2001. In the case of these eight hydroelectric power stations, their reversal to the Ebro Hydrographic Confederation, which precisely manages the concessions, would have to have occurred years ago. The concession periods, as set out in the Waters Act, are seventy-five years and throughout the State are coming to an end historic hydraulic concessions and we therefore urgently need to address what we intend to do with all these plants. In that regard, we were pleased to hear the Minister's words the other day, which sees in this reversal a unique opportunity to be able to review and rethink the role of these hydroelectric exploitations in aspects such as, for example, precisely the management of the basins, the improvement of the ecological state of the water bodies and the contribution of these leaps to the energy transition and the stability of the national electricity system. We also share the view that the hydraulics administration has to assess in the first instance to what extent a exploitation may or may not be financially viable; to what extent, if it is not feasible or if its maintenance is contrary to the public interest, it must require the demolition of the infrastructures that are located there, or to what extent it may be possible to compete in a package of bases, perhaps modulated or changing or according to an update of the general interest, with different prescriptions that allow us to test and facilitate this other type of options taking into account different aspects of the public interest, whether of an environmental, economic or social nature. Progress in the debate is important, but for now everything remains in words and we really need concrete action. That's the problem we're dealing with. On the other hand, one of the conclusions that we have been able to corroborate in the subcommission for water policies and the challenges of climate change is that the increase in water supply and the regularity of contributions will force us, I repeat, to change the current management and governance model, models that at the moment cannot really withstand the climate changes that come upon us. Water is a public domain asset and, as such, must be managed; examples of direct water management models are increasingly being used. Transparency and public control are the models that are really being asked for at the moment, transparency and public control are social demands and we are seeing it precisely on issues such as Catalonia, as mentioned in the past debates. We cannot leave in the hands of markets and speculative management models such a precious and increasingly scarce asset. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2018-12-12-CD181212.u321	President, colleagues, the title of the motion unites two groups of words - general interest and public management - which do not always succeed by going together; in fact, I believe that linking these two groups of words can continually lead to error as to how different projects work or can succeed. Nor will the territorial conflict be resolved, nor will there be any insularity between the territories, because in order to resolve the territorial conflict in Spain, a new system of autonomous financing would have to be put in place and it is not being done. Entering the situation of hydroelectric plants in Spain, we know that there are concessions for seventy-five years, we know that recently they are coming to an end and we know that we are facing a completely uncertain horizon in terms of management. Hydraulics is a vital source for our energy system, it is also an economic activity and is also environmentally sustainable. Hydropower production, for colleagues who do not know, accounts for almost 20% of our country's installed power, and in the average hydraulic years it has reached 15% of our country's energy demand coverage. That is why we believe it is a technology of vital importance, but we also have our reluctance to how it has been managed so far. Hydraulics do not assume a variable cost, the raw material - water - of public management, is free, however it is a technology that can define the price in the marginal market; in fact, in the last five months this technology has been the one that has set the price at 50% of the cases. We therefore believe that it is important that all variants be addressed and, among them, that, despite not being a broadcasting source, it is benefiting from these allowances, since the price that is ultimately fixed is fixed by fossil fuel sources which are actually aggravating the allowances. Therefore, when we approach the problem of our mix it has to be a little more open and much more complex than it seems here. For the market, as I say, there is generally a need for future signals for technologies to be able to invest or project their investment. What happens is that the situation of hydraulics is totally different, in the short and medium term no one plans to make such investments, with which the forecast is not so necessary if we talk about this particular source. We think it is right that an analysis process should be opened at the time to the improvement of hydraulic management, in which we agree. We also agree that it is necessary to check that there is no bad practice in the dealership, how it is managed or whether or not water is retained to set a price. But we do not agree that it should be done in a radical way and thinking that the only solution for this is public management, in that we do not agree. This motion, as I say, gives everything or almost everything to public administration. We also see in this motion that information is requested - we believe it is important that there is always information and transparency - which is already public, is published and is data managed by the Ministry of Ecological Transition. What we propose here is that it conflicts precisely with the regulation of the European Union. A State cannot be a judge and a party, a State cannot be a regulator and at the same time a part of the energy business. We therefore believe that it links or violates that Community law or that Community regulation. As regards the ways in which concessions are to be made, as I say, new specifications must be developed for new concessions or for new renovation projects when these expire, but always without violating the regulation already established from Europe. For us it is impossible for the control of energy prices to be part of or decided by the Government or the autonomous communities. We are in a market where we cannot end up managing or saying 'this is going to cost this' simply because the state at that time considers it or any autonomous community. As I say, in this motion we see a glimpse of the will to create chiringuitos over and over again; that is, to continue to increase the expenditure of the Administration or to allocate public resources to manage something for which the State is currently not prepared. We agree to review the specifications, we agree that it is rigorous with the use and misuse of concessions, although we believe that public management is not the option that guarantees this. But to express our position we will wait for the text to come out definitively tomorrow of the compromise amendment that we believe is being made by the rest of the parties. As I said, we have many points in common, but there are key points on which we do not agree and let us hope that tomorrow they will be resolved. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

I will, of course, ask both the People's Party and the Socialist Party and anyone else who is part of the construction of these employment policies to take into account those that have been successful. In other words, there is a way to find solutions to certain problems and that of those people who are not only in an unemployment situation but who are in sociological places with greater difficulty in finding employment must have solutions found precisely by the nearest institutions. It has been shown that autonomous policies in this regard have been more effective than other State policies. That is why we also believe that these employment policies in the State should be directed towards empowering the autonomous communities, as has happened in the context of the Autonomous Community of the Basque Country, for which we are still calling for some questions and will continue to insist even in the absence of Mr Candón. It will never cease to surprise me, the truth, the nature with which they say these things after having been ruling the country for seven years, really not. In 2015 the People's Party passed a law to regulate vocational training for employment and did, of course, unilaterally and without counting anyone, very much like the People's Party. The employer, the representation of workers, parliamentary groups, autonomous communities and other social actors publicly showed their reservations with the text and the possible usefulness it might have. Time sadly proved that they were right, which usually happens when everyone agrees on something but an actor. The new law did not have defined objectives, it did not define whether it sought to train workers for strategic sectors or to increase the training of certificates of professionalism or to reconvert the skills of unemployed people, or a specific objective. Obviously, it also lacked regulatory stability, as the logical consequence when you draw up such an important law on everyone's back is that they end up using the courts, the Constitutional Court and the National Court, and that the employment training system is therefore blocked. In short, in conclusion, all working people in the country along with self-employed people and SMEs pay religiously a percentage of our salary every month to finance vocational training for employment. Half of that money doesn't go to training and, worse, we don't know where it goes. And, in case there is little money coming from Europe to make it easier for younger people to get decent jobs, we use it to subsidise precariousness by paying entrepreneurs every month 430 euros of public money, of all and of all, to save them on salaries. And of course, when this fabulous period of formation ends, to the street and to start again, to seek life. To give an example, the use of languages perfectly explains what I am saying. On the other hand, we must push and prepare for the change of productive model, which is already here. This is going to be a reality sooner or later, so we better get ready. We all know that it is impossible to change a productive model if training is not geared towards it. We are in a country with one of the largest GDP in the world and, therefore, the working people of this country deserve us to live up to it. ParlaMint-ES_2018-12-12-CD181212.u340	Good afternoon. Since I can't be less, you know that I wish you the best of heart and, moreover, as we have already agreed in other forums that are not Congress but in debates and talks, I have to tell you that you are a wonderful person and that I wish you all the luck of the world. [[Applauds]] Now, as my partner Alberto, I'm going to go to the subject. In this motion, the PP basically urges the Government to improve the employability of people by improving their training, their skills, by relying on vocational training for employment, dual training and the Youth Guarantee bonus. Even if it looks like one of those motions that everyone in principle can support, we are not going to support it, and we are not going to do it for four basic reasons. The first of these is for the absolute lack of self-criticism of the People's Party. What surprised me most when I started reading the motion was: When the PSOE ruled..., and they refer to the PSOE government until 2011. They have been forgotten, they have made a temporary parentheses, which from December 2011 to June 2018 ruled the PP, and the People's Party from the Ministry of Employment was unable to design adequate dual training systems, ensuring this compatibility of training and work to reach a stable job, and all its reforms in dual training at the end resulted in underemployment of young workers at zero cost, impoverishment of young workers, without alternative employment and also gradually forgetting training. Because, what did the People's Party do when it said it was betting on dual formation? Very easily, as soon as he entered the Government, in 2012, he reformed article 11, under the pretext that he was going to enhance this dual training and what he did was to destroy the formative purpose of training contracts. And under the mask of regulating dual formation, what he did was create a very different duality. With the application of this article 11 we had, on the one hand, untrained young people who did not obtain any qualifications, despite being recruited with training, nor did they have a stable job and, on the other hand, we had young graduates who worked with those training contracts in already precarious sectors and with lower wages than an ordinary worker. Why did the PP turn dual training into this element of precariousness? On very specific issues. First, because it made the requirements of the training contract more flexible. Because it made it possible to conclude training contracts with qualified workers. Because it allowed the chaining of training contracts. Because it reduced the number of hours of theoretical training in training contracts. Because it abolished the demand for real training, giving priority to distance training, which made it possible for the worker, together with the business practice of dedicating the work intended for theoretical training to effective work, to terminate the training contract without any degree. All this with 100% bonuses that made the entrepreneur believe that training contracts were cheap employment at zero cost. Therefore, bringing us this motion now as advocates of dual formation is a little surprising to us. Not to mention the exaggerated drive of non-employment practices through scholarships; the inability to manage the resources of the youth guarantee and its own young employment strategy; the unemployment rate, which has already been mentioned here, and the abandonment of public employment services, depriving it of policies such as counselling or guidance, all closely linked to training. What you did is destroy the dual information. Nor did they provide much in terms of training for workers over the age of 45. With labour reform, with the easing of the causes of dismissal, many workers of this age went to the street. There are no specific retraining programmes or training actions for relocation, despite the fact that there were funds from the European Union. In addition, they were left unprotected by abolishing the allowance for over fifty-two years of age. The second reason why we are not going to support this is why my colleague has already pointed out, because there are issues that are already under way, such as the law on vocational training for employment and the national plan for the training of workers for digital tasks. The third reason is that we have always been against wage supplementation in training contracts, because they deepen precariousness, wage devaluation, introduce elements of discrimination and, as Europe says, generate social dumping and do not deepen the quality of employment. Our proposal is to increase the minimum wage, to control the requirements of training contracts and thus to ensure that workers with a training contract have genuine dual training and a decent wage. Finally, and the fourth reason why we are not going to support his motion, is because this Government has already drawn up a youth employment shock plan within the framework of the social dialogue, with multiple actions relating to training through training programmes in key competences and digital competences; through training programmes of commitment to recruitment; through specific training on equal terms to avoid horizontal segregation and the wage gap; in practices to improve the management of the youth guarantee; enhancement of genuine dual training by controlling training contracts and for learning; bet by guidance and specialized itineraries as an essential element and addressing a status of non-work practices. Of course, this Government is going to work for quality employment, for training, for the qualification and occupation of the most vulnerable groups and also for their protection, recovering the allowance for over fifty-two years of age. It seems to us that this type of motion, not recognizing the deterioration that dual formation has suffered with you and if you continue to blame the Socialist Party that ruled in the period before yours, does not make much sense. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
